## Quantitative/statistical relationship inventory

### SP1-01 — Plan identity and statistical precedence

[Cover](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=1), [statistical precedence](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=60): master protocol v5.0, 12/15/2022. Section11 describes recommendations; regimen deviations allowed in R-SAP. M-SAP AppendixI takes precedence over this protocol where they differ; R-SAP sets hierarchy for any R-SAP difference. RSAs specify primary endpoint, maximum sample size, dose-arm allocation and early stopping specifics (p24). Matching main-paper key: design/analysis prespecification and regimen-C trial period. This default version alone must not be treated as the final regimen-specific specification.

### SP1-02 — Label dictionary

Pp9–10 define ALSFRS-R total functional score, SVC slow vital capacity, FVC forced vital capacity, HHD hand-held dynamometry, PAV permanent assisted ventilation, ITT intent-to-treat, N generally participants, ATE extension, NfL biomarker. Matching keys: main/supplement outcome names, population denominators and effect labels.

### SP1-03 — Allocation/intervention design

[Summary](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=12), [allocation](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=34): first equal-probability assignment among available regimens to which participant has not previously been randomized; next3:1 active:matching placebo. Multiple doses allocated as RSA prescribes. Active/placebo blinded, regimen assignment open, blind maintained through ATE. Perpetual adaptive phase2/3 platform, randomized placebo-controlled, maximum24week placebo period; ATE/follow-up duration RSA-specific. No CNM-Au8 dose is printed in this shard. Repeated p20/27/34/42/60. Matching main-paper key: 3:1 regimen-C allocation, pooled active doses, concurrent shared placebo.

### SP1-04 — Primary and secondary endpoint hierarchy

[Summary](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=12), [table](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=25): default primary change baseline through24weeks in ALSFRS-R total score AND survival. Secondary change baseline through24weeks in SVC, muscle strength HHD and grip, and survival. Table repeats endpoint identities without separate time wording. No tertiary designation or regimen-specific key-secondary hierarchy appears here. Matching main-paper keys: primary DRR function/survival, SVC change, HHD/grip, survival.

Pp13,25–26: safety TEAE/SAE, labs, ECG abnormalities, suicidal ideation/behavior; exploratory changes in biofluid biomarkers and PROs. P53 blood biomarker collection all participants; urine baseline8/16/24 up to10mL; DNA optional; CSF RSA-specific. Matching main-paper keys: NfL and PRO status, adverse-event populations/tables. Laboratory units p49 include eGFR MDRD and CrCl Cockcroft-Gault; clinical measurements kg/lb,mmHg,/minute,°C/°F and one-time height cm/in.

### SP1-08 — Trial population boundaries

[Eligibility](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=29), pp30–31,71–72. Possible/probable/lab-supported/definite ALS under revised El Escorial, age≥18, weakness onset≤36months at screening, VC≥50% predicted SVC or in-person FVC if pandemic restrictions; swallow pills/liquids and expected continuing ability. Riluzole stable≥30days; edaravone≥1cycle typically14days; Relyvrio/Albrioza≥30days. ALT/AST>3ULN or eGFR<30mL/min/1.73m² exclusion; specified malignancy exceptions or≥3years recurrence-free. Prior investigational treatment washout max(30days,5half-lives); prior gene therapy exclusion; contraception3months after discontinuation or RSA. Additional RSA criteria permitted. Definition onset is first weakness, not necessarily diagnosis date. El Escorial definite3regions,probable≥2regions rostral UMN; lab-supported clinical1region and EMG≥2limbs; possible combined1region or UMN≥2regions. Matching main-paper keys: enrollment population and prebaseline disease duration/slope.

### SP1-09 — Enrollment, randomized/treated distinctions and discontinuation

Pp32,35,42,47: master-consented=enrolled, irrespective of eventual randomization; record all randomized irrespective of drug receipt. ITT off-treatment follow-up encouraged to24weeks and ATE with minimum ALSFRS-R,AE,medication,C-SSRS/other outcomes. ≥28days off-drug ITT follow-up may replace extra safety call. Drug cessation is distinct from follow-up termination/consent withdrawal. Early-termination measures RSA-dependent. Attempted-failed/illness-disability missingness recorded distinctly from absent procedures. Matching main-paper keys: enrolled/randomized/treated/analyzed flow denominators and missingness.

### SP1-10 — Repeat participants and analysis unit

[Rescreening](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=43), [analysis](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=63). Completion or early success/futility permits rescreen after max(30days,5half-lives). Personal/adverse-event discontinuation cannot rescreen until would-be24week date. Time-dependent screen failure can rescreen after resolution; regimen eligibility failure permits immediate different assignment. Repeat participant treated as new participant/new baseline and new observation set per regimen; across-regimen sets independent conditional on baseline covariates, adjusting prebaseline ALSFRS-R slope and post-washout progression. Matching keys: participant versus randomization counts, repeat entries, independence assumptions.

### SP1-11 — Survival endpoint, PAV and ascertainment

[Definition](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=60), pp15,48,64. Primary survival composite death OR PAV, PAV noninvasive or invasive mechanical ventilation >22hours/day for >7consecutive days; date first consecutive day. Primary24week period; vital status date death/PAV/last alive/last PAV-free, checked throughout placebo-controlled follow-up and again at LPLV for all randomized; later mortality publicly sourced permitted. Death-equivalent safety KM. Matching keys: death/PAV/tracheostomy composites, long-term mortality, survival time horizon and endpoint status.

### SP1-12 — ITT and shared control selection

[Primary population](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=61), p62. All randomized regimen-active, regimen placebo and contributing-regimen placebo. Shared control includes own concurrent randomized placebo, other concurrent and previous-regimen nonconcurrent controls. Concurrent defined within6month=180day window of first and last analysis-regimen randomization. Additional major RSA eligibility may subset controls only if assessed across platform and prespecified RSA. Available ALSFRS-R0/4/8/12/16/20/24 plus substitute/early-termination observations during placebo period and survival within24weeks. Matching keys: primary full analysis set and concurrent/shared definitions; preserve recommended nature and regimen-specific precedence.

### SP1-13 — Joint Bayesian primary model and effect identity

[Model](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=62), p60. Bayesian shared-parameter repeated-measures ALSFRS-R plus exponential proportional-hazards survival. Common effect links slowing in function/survival. Functional placebo linear progression, active proportional slowing at each time point. DRR is active-relative-to-placebo ratio of disease-decline rates (ALSFRS-R and mortality rate); describes proportional slowing. Mortality informs beyond censored functional data, extent depending mortality rate. Matching keys: reported DRR, credible interval and posterior probability; do not relabel as simple crude ratio.

### SP1-14 — Primary model covariates/random effects

P62: participant random baseline intercept and progression slope; participant-specific baseline covariates affect progression; regimen-specific random slopes address shared-control differences beyond included covariates. Full specification M-SAP AppendixI. Prebaseline ALSFRS-R slope adjustment named p63 for repeat participants. No complete covariate list/priors here; map them from assigned SAP shards instead of inferring. Matching keys: adjustment, placebo pooling/random effects, prebaseline slope.

### SP1-15 — Missingness and sensitivity analysis

Pp62,64: primary uses all longitudinal observations accommodates unequal follow-up, death through survival component, MAR reasons unrelated to progression; no primary imputation. Planned sensitivities investigate missingness influence on estimates/inference, model assumptions and varying shared-control/analysis-population definitions. Details M-SAP AppendixI; no discrete named subgroup list in this shard. Matching keys: prespecified sensitivity populations, missing-data implementation, functional/mortality separate models.

### SP1-16 — Multiple active doses

[Multiple doses](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=63): recommended pooled all active doses versus shared control primary; individual-dose secondary analyses in R-SAP. RSA dictates dosage, changes, compliance and allocation. No numeric CNM-Au8 dose stated on pp1–76. Matching keys: pooled30/60mg primary, dose-specific secondary results; numbers of those doses must come from other supplied units.

### SP1-17 — Interim, success, futility and decision rules

[Design](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=27), pp60–64. Begin platform interims when≥1regimen has40 randomized participants (p63 explicitly30active10control) with opportunity≥24weeks follow-up. Global interims every12weeks. Regimen eligible when sufficient data40/24weeks. Final when all randomized have opportunity24weeks if no early stop. Default early futility only; early success only specified RSA, posterior superiority threshold set M-SAP AppendixI simulations to overall one-sided type I error2.5%. Futility posterior Pr(slowing≥10%)<5%. Criteria nonbinding. Safety stopping possible but no numeric safety boundary here. Matching keys: prespecified efficacy posterior decision threshold, observed interim/finality and futility.

### SP1-18 — Sample size and simulation operating characteristics

[Sample design](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=60), [simulation](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=65). Recommended160 randomized/regimen,3:1→120active40placebo. Simulation inputs placebo ALSFRS-R progression, measurement error,mortality,effects on function/survival,dropout,accrual; virtually executes interims across scenarios. First3regimens examples: null one-sided type I error2.4% per regimen,average duration14months,28% early futility. Common function/survival effects: slowing25/30/35% gives power61/77/88%,respectively. Functional30% slowing without survival benefit power72%; with negative survival effect power68%. These scenarios<1% early futility. Full numerical assumptions/simulation details delegated M-SAP AppendixI. Matching keys: claimed trial power, planned160 and30% slowing assumptions. Powers are simulated rather than simple fixed two-arm formula outputs.

### SP1-19 — ALSFRS-R scoring and direction

[Measure](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=51), [scale start](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=73), pp74–75. Twelve activities0–4 each,total0–48,higher better. Speech,salivation,swallowing,handwriting,cutting food (5a without gastrostomy OR alternate5b with gastrostomy),dressing/hygiene,turning bed,walking,stairs,dyspnea,orthopnea,respiratory insufficiency. 5a/5b alternatives do not add a thirteenth item. Respiratory insufficiency4none,3intermittent BiPAP,2nightly continuous,1day+night continuous,0invasive ventilation/intubation/tracheostomy. Orthopnea3 generally≤2pillows,2>2pillows,1only sitting,0mechanical assistance; dyspnea4none→0considering support. Each functional item's direction4best→0least. Reliability>0.88 quoted background; phone/caregiver/web measures allowed. Matching keys: functional slope/total, bulbar domains, direction.

### SP1-20 — SVC derivation and measurement units

[Measure](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=52), p41. Upright SVC approved spirometer face mask.3trials required,up to5 if highest-v-second≥10% for initial3; only3best recorded. Highest eligibility VC;≥3measurable post-screening. Predicted and percent-predicted VC use Quanjer GLI equations. In-person FVC may serve screening eligibility only; post-screening unobtainable SVC skipped. Matching keys: respiratory percent-predicted change and baseline eligibility≥50%.

### SP1-21 — Muscle strength endpoints

P52: six proximal groups bilaterally (shoulder flexion,elbow flexion/extension,hip flexion,knee flexion/extension), plus bilateral wrist extension,abductor pollicis brevis,abductor digiti minimi,first dorsal interosseous contraction,ankle dorsiflexion. Bilateral Jamar grip maximum isometric strength in pounds. HHD total/composite derivation not specified here. Matching keys: strength composite/unit and grip secondary.

### SP1-22 — Safety population and quantities

[Safety analysis](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=64), p65; pp55–59/67–68 definitions. Safety all initiators, by treatment group, deaths/death-equivalents,TEAE,vitals/ECG/labs,ideation/behavior. Continuous observed values plus baseline change each time; time-to-death/death-equivalent KM. AEs both event counts and proportion of participants with each TEAE by MedDRA organ class/high-level/preferred term, severity,relatedness,outcome. Lab CTCAE grade and below/within/above normal, baseline-to-visit shift tables; clinically significant out-of-range labs in TEAE summaries. AE monitoring from master consent, including worsened ALS symptoms but excluding separately captured SVC/ALSFRS-R/strength scores; stable chronic conditions not AEs. Elective PEG/routine admission alone not SAE. Aggregate death/respiratory failure/routine hospitalization against concurrent placebo reviewed DSMB. Matching keys: treated versus randomized safety denominators, participants versus number of events.

### SP1-23 — C-SSRS categories and time window

Pp50–51 and [baseline questionnaire](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=76). Baseline lifetime,then since-last-visit. Ideation questions1wish dead,2nonspecific active thoughts,3methods without intent,4some intent without specific plan,5plan+intent; if1&2negative skip behavior section;if2yes ask3–5;if1or2yes rate intensity for most severe type1–5. Frequency scores1<weekly,2weekly,3two-to-five/week,4daily/almost,5many/day. Duration1seconds/minutes,2<1hour,3one-to-four hours,4four-to-eight hours,5>8hours/persistent. Positive4/5 or suicidal-behavior response triggers clinician assessment. Questionnaire continues outside this shard; no coverage claimed beyond76. Matching keys: safety suicidal ideation/behavior frequencies and treatment-emergent status.

### SP1-24 — Planned joint-rank secondary efficacy analysis

[Joint rank](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=64): rank each participant pair first survival during24week placebo period, then if both survived or one censored before other observed event, baseline-to-last jointly observed ALSFRS-R change up to24weeks. Analyze summed ranks by linear regression adjusting important covariates. Other secondary/exploratory models delegated RSA/R-SAP. Matching keys: CAFS/joint-rank planned versus reported analysis, adjusted test.

## Prospective relationships

### SP3-N001 — Allocation and sample size

[p163](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=163), pp186,188. Approximately 160 randomized participants. Allocation 3:1 active:placebo, explicitly 120:40. Active participants equally split: 60 at 30 mg/day and 60 at 60 mg/day. Approximately 60 US centers is an administrative detail. Matching key: randomized count and dose allocation.

### SP3-S001 — Enrollment stopping

[p163](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=163). Enrollment stops when predefined regimen futility criteria are met or target randomization reached. No futility probability threshold appears on this page. Matching key: stopping rule.

### SP3-N002 — Treatment and follow-up duration

[p163](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=163), pp164,188. Maximum placebo-controlled treatment 24 weeks. Screening 6 weeks, safety follow-up4 weeks, total up to 34 weeks/about 8 months and approximately 10 visits. Follow-up call 28 days after last dose unless entering OLE. Matching key: randomized intervention duration and outcome horizon.

### SP3-N003 — Placebo-controlled visit windows

[p164](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=164), pp165–166. Master screening −42 to −1 days; regimen screening −41 to 0; baseline day 0; W 2 day 14±3; W 4 day 28±7; W 8 day 56±7; W 12 day 84±3; W 16 day 112±7; W 20 day 140±3; W 24/early termination day 168±7. Follow-up28±3 days after last dose. Maximum interval between visits 64 days. Matching key: endpoint observation windows.

### SP3-N004 — Assessment schedule

[p164](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=164), pp165–166. ALSFRS-R at screening, baseline, W 4,W 8,W 12,W 16,W 20,W 24. SVC at screening, baseline, W 8,W 16,W 24. Muscle strength at baseline,W 8,W 16,W 24. CNS-BFS at baseline,W 8,W 16,W 24. ALSAQ-40 schedule row at baseline,W 24. Voice at baseline,W 4,W 8,W 16,W 24 and twice weekly at home. ECG at screening,W 24. Safety labs at screening,baseline,W 4,W 8,W 16,W 24. Matching key: outcome trajectories and PRO schedules.

### SP3-S002 — Vital-status definition and ascertainment

[p166](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=166), footnote 13. Vital status means date of death/death equivalent or last known alive. Determine for every randomized participant at end of placebo-controlled follow-up, generally W 24; if alive then, ascertain again at regimen last patient last visit. Later public-source ascertainment is allowed under master §8.15. Matching key: survival definition and follow-up after week 24.

### SP3-N005 — Sampling and screening adaptations

[p166](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=166), footnotes 12,15,18. Predose whole blood/plasma at baseline,W 24; predose plasma at W 4,W 8. Optional CSF collection at W 16 may move to W 24 for logistics. Pandemic screening eligibility may use FVC measured by pulmonary lab or approved home spirometer, or sustained phonation. Matching key: biomarker sampling and respiratory eligibility.

### SP3-S003 — Primary efficacy endpoint/model

[p184](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=184). Primary objective: CNM-Au8 versus placebo on ALS progression. Primary endpoint: change in ALSFRS-R total score using a Bayesian repeated-measures model accounting for loss to follow-up due to mortality. Matching key: primary functional/survival analysis and model.

### SP3-S004 — Secondary outcome status

[p184](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=184). Secondary efficacy endpoints are respiratory function change by SVC; muscle-strength change by isometric HHD and grip strength; survival. These are printed as secondary endpoints. Matching key: secondary endpoint hierarchy.

### SP3-S005 — Safety outcome status

[p184](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=184). Safety endpoints: treatment-emergent adverse and serious adverse events; laboratory changes and treatment-emergent clinically significant abnormalities; ECG changes and treatment-emergent clinically significant abnormalities; suicidal ideation and behavior. Matching key: safety definitions and grouping.

### SP3-S006 — Exploratory outcome status and threshold

[p184](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=184), p185. Exploratory efficacy endpoints: quantitative voice changes; active-versus-placebo difference in proportion experiencing ≥6-point ALSFRS-R decline from baseline to W 24; neurodegeneration biofluid biomarkers; patient-reported outcomes; respiratory change by home spirometry. No death component appears in the printed ≥6-point endpoint definition here. Matching key: responder threshold, composite components, PRO/voice/biomarker hierarchy.

### SP3-N006 — Intervention and eligibility

[p186](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=186), pp188–191. Multicenter randomized placebo-controlled trial of oral daily 30 mg or 60 mg CNM-Au8 versus color-matched placebo. Riluzole and edaravone permitted concomitantly. Master eligibility applies; additional regimen exclusion is allergy to gold, gold salts or colloidal gold. Double-blind treatment approximately 24 weeks. Matching key: intervention and eligible population.

### SP3-N007 — Dose, composition and administration

[p189](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=189), pp171,190–191. Table 2: 30 mg arm=250μg/mL×60 mL×2 bottles/day; 60 mg arm=500μg/mL×60 mL×2; placebo=2×60 mL. All daily volume 120 mL, oral once daily at same time±1 hour, at least 30 min before food. Table 3 per 60 mL bottle: NaHCO 3 32.8 mg; Au 15 mg or 30 mg. OLE 120 mL bottle: NaHCO 3 65.5 mg, Au 30 mg. Gastric tube administration allowed, with 20–30 mL water flush before/after. Matching key: randomized dose and administration.

### SP3-N008 — Dose changes and OLE version distinction

[p192](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=192). Tolerability-related reduction to one bottle/day only with medical monitor approval; two-bottle rechallenge may be approved; remain at one bottle if rechallenge not tolerated; no drug holidays. OLE initially maintains blinded active dose, with former placebo participants re-randomized to 30 mg or 60 mg. After topline blinded-phase results, OLE dose adjusted to 30 mg following Clene/sponsor consultation; original dose assignment remains blinded. Matching key: dose adaptation and OLE versus randomized phase.

### SP3-S007 — Compliance/per-protocol rule

[p196](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=196). Overall treatment compliance for per-protocol analysis set should be 80%–120% of planned dose. Bottle counts at clinic visits; compliance checks during phone visits. Noncompliance may lead to discontinuation after investigator/monitor consultation. Matching key: per-protocol population rule.

### SP3-S008 — Completion, withdrawal and OLE eligibility

[p186](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=186), p203. Placebo-controlled participation ends after planned visits on/off drug, early termination plus safety call, or consent withdrawal/loss to follow-up. Drug discontinuation with continued protocol participation does not require early-termination visit/safety call. OLE eligibility described as completion of placebo-controlled trial on drug. OLE runs until primary 24 week results available or support/development terminated. Matching key: follow-up population and OLE eligibility.

### SP3-N009 — OLE observation windows

[p167](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=167), pp204–207. OLE schedule days 14+3,28±7,56±7,84±3,112±7,140±3,168±3,196±14, then every 12 weeks±14 days; safety call 28±7 days after last dose. Narrative p204 prints W 2=14±3 and W 4=28±10; p205 W 8=56±7,W 12=84±3; p206 W 16=112±7,W 20=140±3,W 24=168±3; p207 W 28=196±14. These location-specific windows are preserved without diagnosis. Matching key: OLE timepoints, separate from randomized outcomes.

### SP3-N010 — OLE assessments and maximum intervals

[p168](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=168), pp191,204–207. Maximum visit interval 64 days through W 16,96 days thereafter. OLE biomarkers atW 16,W 28,W 52, regimen-specific collection every 24 weeks thereafter. ALSAQ-40 atW 28 and every 24 weeks thereafter. ALSFRS-R atW 4,W 8,W 12,W 16,W 20,W 24,W 28/Q 12; SVC/home spirometry atW 4,W 8,W 16,W 28/Q 12; CNS-BFS atW 8,W 16,W 28/Q 12. Matching key: OLE assessment schedule.

### SP3-N011 — Narrative regimen visits

[p201](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=201), pp200–203. Baseline day 0 includes ALSAQ-40,CNS-BFS,home spirometry,voice and PK/PD. W 4/W 8 voice and plasmaPK, home spirometry/CNS-BFS only W 8. W 16 includes ALSAQ-40,home spirometry,CNS-BFS,voice. W 24/early termination includes PROs,home spirometry,voice,whole-bloodPK/PD,plasmaPD,urinePD. No regimen-specific procedures at W 2,W 12,W 20 phone visits. Safety call 28±3 days after last dose. Matching key: PRO/sampling schedule.

### SP3-N012 — Laboratory alerts

[p198](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=198). ALT/AST>3×ULN; creatinine>1.5×baseline; platelet count<75,000/mm³. Alerts informative only, without mandatory dose reduction/discontinuation. Investigator determines follow-up/management. Matching key: safety assessment definitions.

### SP3-S009 — Remote-visit adaptations and missing procedures

[p199](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=199), p204. Remote W 4,W 8,W 16 permitted for pandemic/other safety needs. Voice remotely; home spirometry/CNS-BFS atW 8,W 16. No home-health PK/PD blood collection; record missing procedures. OLE remote options includeW 4,W 8,W 16,W 28,W 40; no remote blood/urine PK/PD. Matching key: remote assessments/missing data context.

### SP3-N013 — Assessment instruments

[p208](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=208), p209. Voice twice weekly:5 fixed and 5 random sentences, consonant-vowel sequence, sustained phonation, single-breath counting. English-only app, no caregiver language assistance. ALSAQ-40 has 40 self-report questions; CNS-BFS has 21 questions in 3 domains (swallowing,speech,salivation); caregiver help allowed with both. Home FVC uses MIR Spirobank Smart with video/phone coaching and 3–5 maneuvers. Matching key: voice/PRO/respiratory methods.

### SP3-S010 — PK/PD analyses and first 40 review

[p210](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=210), p211. Predose whole-blood Au PK, separate PK analysis plan. Riluzole population PK under separate plan: first 40 participants taking riluzole randomized toRegimenC and reachingW 8 reviewed by DSMB/unblinded PK designee for CNM-Au8-versus-placebo changes atW 4/W 8. Predose plasma/whole blood/urine PD; separate PD analysis plan. Matching key: PK interim review and biomarker plans.

### SP3-S011 — Statistical design and success interim

[p212](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=212). Regimen follows default master AppendixI design with one deviation: no interim analyses for early success. Operating characteristics quantified by clinical-trial simulation; details in regimen SAP. No power or decision-threshold numbers printed in this shard. Matching key: success interim and operating characteristics.

### SP3-S012 — Shared controls

[p212](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=212). Primary analysis includes sharing all controls from other regimens, justified by minor eligibility differences and no expected systematic primary-endpoint differences. No borrowing-weight formula appears here. Matching key: primary control population.

### SP3-N014 — ALSAQ-40 questionnaire

[p213](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=213), pp214–221. Recall last 2 weeks;40 numbered items; one choice per question, Never/Rarely/Sometimes/Often/Always. Functional items add “cannot perform” to Always. No numeric score conversion, missing-item rule or formula printed. Page item ranges: p214 items 1–5 walking/falls/balance; p215 items 6–10 walking pain/stairs/standing/transfers; p216 items 11–15 arm-hand use/bed turning/objects/books/writing; p217 items 16–20 housework/feeding/grooming/dressing/washing; p218 items 21–25 swallowing/solids/liquids/conversation/comprehensibility; p219 items 26–30 stuttering/speaking/less speech/frustration/self-consciousness; p220 items 31–35 loneliness/boredom/embarrassment/hopelessness/burden; p221 items 36–40 continuing/anger/depression/future/independence. Matching key: PRO scale/recall.

### SP3-N015 — CNS-BFS scale

[p222](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=222), p223. Seven sialorrhea items, seven speech, seven swallowing. Responses 1=Does not apply,2=Rarely,3=Occasionally,4=Frequently,5=Most of the time; speech additionally 6=Unable to communicate by speaking. Separate 3 domain totals and overall score blanks; feeding-tube checkbox. Derived all-item total possible 21–112 from 7×5+7×6+7×5; this range is derived, not a printed normative range. No actual participant score. Matching key: PRO component scoring.

### SP3-N016 — Particle characteristics

[p172](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=172), Table 1. Median diameter 13 nm; disc/spherical aspect 0.2/1.0; volume 2.3×10²/1.2×10³nm³; surface 3.2×10²/5.3×10²nm²; atoms 1.4×10⁴/6.8×10⁴; molecular weight 2.7×10³/1.3×10⁴kDa; total surface per mL 3.6×10²/1.2×10²cm²; particles per mL 1.1×10¹⁴/2.3×10¹³; particles per 60 mL 3.4×10¹⁵/6.8×10¹⁴. Narrative p171 gives 13,000–66,000 atoms and 100–500 trillion particles/mL at 500μg/mL. Not clinical trial results.

### SP3-N017 — Dose-justification tables and Figure 10

[p194](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=194), [p195](../../../joi240158supp1_prod_1742927563.7611.pdf#page=195). Table 4 human 60 kg doses 15/30/60/90 mg correspond to 9.3/18.5/37.0/55.5 mg/m². Rat 21 day NOAEL 40 mg/kg/day=240 mg/m², margins 25.9/13.0/6.5/4.3; canine 90 mg/kg/day=1800 mg/m², margins 194.6/97.3/48.6/32.4. Table 5 human 21 day AUC 32.3/41.4/50.3/66.0 ng·h/mL; rat 40 mg/kg/day AUC 106, ratios 3.3/2.6/2.1/1.6; canine 90 mg/kg/day AUC 596, ratios 18.5/14.4/11.8/9.0. Table 6 chronic rat 6 months 40 mg/kg/day AUC 209, ratios 6.5/5.0/4.2/3.2; canine 9 months 10 mg/kg/day AUC 440, ratios 13.6/10.6/8.7/6.7. Animal AUC footnote averages male/female end-of-study values. Figure 10 labels Day 21 whole-blood Au AUC 0–24 geometric means 30.6 rodent 10 mg/kg,32.3 human 15 mg,41.4 human 30 mg,50.3 human 60 mg,66.0 human 90 mg. These are background exposure comparisons.

## Numeric tables and visual transcriptions

All following comparisons are active relative to placebo. Table values are simulated operating characteristics or illustrative simulated trials in PLAN. No inferential screening or candidate diagnosis is performed.

### SP4-N001 — Simulation parameters, pp46–47

Base/sensitivity a/sensitivity b: accrual participants/month40/20/60; non-mortality dropout per month2%/4%/6%; otherregimen enrollment starts1/3/6-months apart; ALSFRS slopeallregimens PROACT/10% slowerandlessvariable/10% fasterandmorevariable; measurementerrorallregimens PROACT/10%less/10%more; acrossregimen slope none/focal10% slower/focal10% faster; acrossregimenmeasurementerror none/focal10%less/focal10%more;24-weekmortality5%/10%/20%. ### SP4-N010 — Numeric field definitions without observed trial results

All baseline/safety summaries, laboratory thresholds,randomization ratios,visit windows,formula scale conversions,imputation bounds,subgroup cutpoints,priors and operating characteristic probabilities are indexed in SP4-S002–SP4-S034 and the complete page text below. There is no denominator from the observed CNM-Au8 trial in pp1–65. The simulated mortality Ev/N arrays distinguish cumulative observed events from risk-set attrition and functional-contributionN.

## Page-by-page mapping ledger

Each source page is explicitly recorded below with a complete native/layout transcription locator. Pages1,6,7,31,62,63,64,65 are chiefly cover/TOC/references/signatures/version administration: no observed quantitative outcome;version fields apply asindexedabove. Pages2–5 revisionhistory are applicableplanversion/analysis-change evidence. Pages10–11 schedule/footnotes apply to windowsandfollow-up. Everyotherpage containsstatistical methods,measurementdefinitions,simulation assumptions/results,or primaryanalysis design; allmappedabove. Freshimageinspection supplements pp54,55,58,59,60. No page is omitted or sampled.

```text
Cudkowicz (PI) HEALEY ALS Platform Trial: Master Statistical Analysis Plan     Version 3.0, 06 Feb 2023


1. Governing Documents
This Master Statistical Analysis Plan (M-SAP) defines the default outcome measures, analysis
samples, and planned analyses for the placebo-controlled portion of regimens tested in the
HEALEY ALS Platform Trial for Regimen-specific Appendices (RSAs) initiated after
implementation of version 5.0 of the HEALEY ALS Platform Trial Master Protocol in February
2023. RSAs initiated on earlier versions of the HEALEY ALS Platform Trial Master Protocol
will refer to version 2.0 of the M-SAP dated 06 Mar 2020. Each RSA will have a regimen-
specific SAP (R-SAP) that specifies any deviations or further details that supersede the
specifications given here. The M-SAP and each R-SAP supplement the Master Protocol, the
"ALS Master Protocol Recommended Statistical Analysis, Design and Simulation Report"
(MPRDR, Appendix 1 to this M-SAP), and the applicable RSA. Please refer to the Master
Protocol and applicable RSA for details on the rationale for the study design, eligibility criteria,
conduct of the trial, clinical assessments and schedule of assessments, definitions and reporting
of adverse events, data management conventions, and regulatory oversight and compliance
procedures. The MPRDR and any regimen-specific deviations described in the applicable RSA
and R-SAP are authoritative in defining the primary and interim analyses. In case of
discrepancies between either SAP and the Master Protocol and applicable RSA concerning other
matters of analysis, the M-SAP and applicable R-SAP are authoritative. In case of discrepancies
between the M-SAP and R-SAP, the R-SAP is authoritative. On all other matters, the Master
Protocol and applicable RSA are authoritative.

2. Study Design

2.1       Overview
The HEALEY ALS Platform Trial is a perpetual multi-center, multi-regimen clinical trial
evaluating the safety and efficacy of investigational products for the treatment of ALS.
Investigational products will be evaluated according to details specified in distinct RSAs. Each
RSA will describe the nature of the intervention and its mechanism of action, including the mode
and frequency of administration, number of doses, the specific target population (to be selected
within the pre-defined subsets of the Master Protocol), additional enrollment criteria (if any),
sample size, and other specific intervention-related information and assessments (safety or other
assessments that may be in addition to those outlined in the Master Protocol).

2.2       Study Objectives
Primary Efficacy Objective:
      x    To evaluate the efficacy of multiple investigational products as compared to placebo on
           ALS disease progression.
Secondary Efficacy Objectives:
      x    To evaluate the effect of multiple investigational products on selected secondary
           measures of disease progression.
Safety Objectives:
      x    To evaluate the safety of multiple investigational products for people with ALS.
```

```text
Exploratory Efficacy Objectives:
      x    To evaluate the effect of multiple investigational products on selected biomarkers and
           endpoints.
Each regimen will test the efficacy of a distinct treatment.

2.3       Study Population
Individuals eligible for trial participation are men or women at least 18 years old who meet the
El Escorial criteria of possible, laboratory-supported probable, probable, or definite for a
diagnosis of sporadic or familial ALS, report onset of weakness within 36 months of screening,
possess a slow vital capacity (SVC) at least 50% of that expected based on age, sex, and height,
are treatment naïve or on a stable dose of riluzole, edaravone, or both, are naïve to or have
started treatment with sodium phenylbutyrate/taurursodiol at least 30 days prior to Master
Protocol Screening, lack unstable clinical or psychiatric conditions, and were not recently
exposed to other investigational treatments or prior gene therapy. Detailed inclusion and
exclusion criteria are specified in the Master Protocol and applicable RSA.
Participants will be recruited from approximately 60 centers located throughout the US that are
part of the Northeast ALS (NEALS) Consortium.

2.4       Participant Flow
Participants that consent to the Master Protocol will be screened for Master Protocol-level
inclusion and exclusion criteria. Those determined eligible will be randomly assigned to an
applicable regimen. Participants that consent to their assigned regimen will be screened for any
regimen-specific inclusion and exclusion criteria. Those determined eligible will be randomized
to either active or placebo treatment for their assigned regimen.
This perpetual platform trial will continue enrolling research participants for as long as there are
regimens that are enrolling. Enrollment into a specific regimen will continue until the target
number of randomized participants in a regimen has been reached unless stopped earlier due to a
determination of regimen success or futility.
Each regimen will compare double-blind active and placebo treatment groups for a maximum of
up to 28 weeks or as otherwise specified in the RSA. Participants will complete a baseline visit
prior to initiating study drug. After the baseline visit, participants will be seen in clinic at 4, 8,
16, and 24 weeks and will complete telephone visits at 2, 12, and 20 weeks and 28 days after last
dose of study drug or as otherwise specified in the RSA.
An active treatment extension (ATE) will be available to participants who complete the double-
blind phase as described in their respective RSAs. Participants may also opt to re-screen for the
Master Platform and enroll in a second regimen, if eligible.
Detailed descriptions of study procedures and timing are specified in the Master Protocol and
applicable RSA.

2.5       Regimen Allocation
Participants determined eligible for Master Protocol-level inclusion and exclusion criteria will be
randomized with equal probability to all applicable regimens. Applicable regimens are those for
```

```text
2.6      Treatment Allocation
Prior to the baseline visit, eligible participants for a given regimen will be randomly allocated in
a 3:1 ratio to active or placebo treatment. Active participants may be further divided at random to
more than one dose group, if applicable. Randomizations follow a permuted-block schedule,
stratified for all combinations of use vs. non-use of riluzole, edaravone, and sodium
phenylbutyrate/taurursodiol at the time of screening for the Master Protocol. The randomization
schedule will be prepared by computer program by an unblinded study statistician who will not
contribute to decisions concerning the course, conduct, or analysis of the HEALEY ALS
Platform Trial.

2.7      Treatment Administration
Details of treatment administration are described in the applicable RSA.

2.8      Allocation Concealment
Allocation to regimen is not concealed. Allocation to treatment groups within a regimen is
concealed to participants and investigators. The regimen randomization schedule is known only
to the unblinded study statistician who generated the schedule, any unblinded staff responsible
for managing the interactive response technology (IRT) used for randomization, all staff at the
central pharmacy responsible for distribution of study drug, and the unblinded Independent
Statistical Analysis Committee (ISAC) responsible for interim analyses. An independent Data
and Safety Monitoring Board (DSMB) will receive unblinded data as described in the DSMB
Charter. Concealment of the true regimen treatment assignment of specific participants is
achieved by using anonymous study drug kit identifiers linked at time of dispensation to
participants. Member of the HEALEY ALS Platform Trial Executive Committee, members of
the Regimen Steering Committee, site investigators and other site staff including research
pharmacists, clinical coordination and data management staff, the medical monitor, and all
participants are blinded to regimen treatment assignments of participants. Additional details
concerning blinding are described in Data Sharing Plan and the Clintrex/CMSU Firewall Plan.
```

```text
3. General Considerations for Data Analysis

3.1     Statistical Software
Statistical analyses will be performed using SAS (SAS Institute, NC, USA), R (R Foundation for
Statistical Computing, Vienna, Austria), or JAGS (Plummer, SourceForge).

3.2     Summary Statistics
Data will be summarized with respect to disposition, demographics, pre-treatment
characteristics, efficacy endpoints, safety endpoints, and tolerability. Summary statistics for
continuous variables will include the number of observations, the mean, median, standard
deviation, inter-quartile range, and range. Summaries of categorical data will include counts,
denominators, and percentages.

3.3     Precision
Results will generally be reported to 3 significant figures. Percentages will generally be reported
to 0.1 percentage points. P-values will generally be reported to two digits when greater than or
equal to 0.095, to three digits when greater than or equal to 0.00095 and less than 0.095, and as
<0.001 for all smaller values.

3.4     Transformations
Data that are strictly positive, continuous, and strongly right skewed will typically be log-
transformed prior to any inferential testing. Skewness greater than 3 will be used as a guide in
determining which variables to transform. Which variables warrant transformation and how they
will be transformed will be determined prior to regimen data lock. Original, untransformed
values will be used for all summaries. Any data transformations used in the primary and interim
analyses are described in the MPRDR (Appendix 1 to this M-SAP).

3.5     Multiplicity Adjustments
A single primary analysis is planned. The default criterion for significance at the final analysis is
adjusted to ensure an overall one-sided type 1 error rate less than 0.025 in the absence of early
stopping for futility. Secondary efficacy endpoints will be tested in a pre-specified order using a
closed testing procedure to maintain an overall two-tailed family-wise error rate less than 0.05
under the condition that the primary and all secondary analyses are null. Nominal comparison-
wise p-values will also be reported for all analyses unless otherwise specified.

3.6     Missing Data
If baseline values of a given measure are missing, the last observed pre-treatment value will be
used. Missing baseline covariates will be imputed using the mean of the respective covariate. For
any baseline covariate that is transformed for analysis, means will be imputed after
transformation. Off-schedule results, e.g., those collected as part of an Early Termination Visit,
will be used in place of the closest missing scheduled visit (while preserving visit sequence) for
analyses that depend on visit-specific data. Other results will not be carried forward.
```

```text
Cudkowicz (PI) HEALEY ALS Platform Trial: Master Statistical Analysis Plan   Version 3.0, 06 Feb 2023


The planned mixed model analyses, where applied, yield estimates that are unbiased conditional
on the observed values under a missing at random assumption. Secondary analyses of efficacy
endpoints that accommodate missing values are described below (see Sections 6.5.5 and 6.5.6).

4. Study Endpoints

4.1     Efficacy Endpoints
The primary efficacy endpoint is change from baseline through Week 24 in disease severity as
measured by the ALS Functional Rating Scale-Revised (ALSFRS-R) total score and survival.
Secondary efficacy endpoints include change from baseline through Week 24 in muscle strength
as measured isometrically using hand-held dynamometry (HHD) and grip strength, respiratory
function as assessed by slow vital capacity (SVC), and survival.

4.2     Exploratory Efficacy Endpoints
The following categories of exploratory endpoints will be evaluated:
x     Biofluid biomarkers
x     Patient-reported outcomes

4.3     Safety Endpoints
The following safety endpoints will be evaluated:
x     Treatment-emergent adverse and serious adverse events.
x     Changes in laboratory values and treatment-emergent and clinically significant laboratory
      abnormalities.
x     Changes in ECG parameters and treatment-emergent and clinically significant ECG
      abnormalities.
x     Treatment-emergent suicidal ideation and suicidal behavior.
Reported proportions will use as their denominator all participants in the applicable Safety
sample (see Section 6.1 below).

5. Measurement Definitions

5.1     ALSFRS-R
The ALSFRS-R (Cedarbaum et al. 1999) is a 12-item instrument completed by clinician
interview for assessing participants’ function in four domains: bulbar, fine motor, gross motor,
and respiratory. Each item is scored from 0 to 4 with higher scores indicating greater function.
The ALSFRS-R total score is the sum of all items (range 0 to 48). Domain scores are the sum of
their respective items (range 0 to 12): bulbar function = Q1-Q3, fine motor function = Q4-Q6,
gross motor function = Q7-Q9, and respiratory function = QR1-QR3 (Q10-Q12).
The average rate of disease progression prior to the Baseline Visit as measured by ALSFRS-R
(pre-baseline slope of ALSFRS-R or delta-FRS) will be calculated as 48 minus the ALSFRS-R
```

```text
Cudkowicz (PI) HEALEY ALS Platform Trial: Master Statistical Analysis Plan   Version 3.0, 06 Feb 2023


total score measured at the Baseline Visit, that difference divided by the number of months from
onset of symptom weakness due to ALS to the date of the Baseline Visit. The number of months
will be calculated as the difference in days from onset of symptomatic weakness to the Baseline
Visit multiplied by 12 / 365.25. The date of onset of symptomatic weakness will be imputed as
the fifteenth day of a month if not specified more precisely.

5.2    SVC
Slow vital capacity (SVC) is the maximum volume of air that can be slowly exhaled after slow,
maximal inhalation. Trained technicians coach participants through 3 to 5 maneuvers using a
spirometer. A minimum of 2 completed SVC maneuvers are required to calculate an estimate of
maximum SVC for a given participant visit. A single maximum vital capacity reported by a
pulmonary function lab and obtained by either a slow or forced maneuver is also acceptable. The
maximum volume expired is converted to percent of predicted normal using normal values for
forced vital capacity (FVC). FVC normal values are calculated based on sex, age, height, and
ethnicity using equations published by the Global Lung Function Initiative (GLI, Quanjer et al.
2012). Higher values indicate greater respiratory function.
Age will be calculated as number of days from date of birth to the date of a given SVC
assessment divided by 365.25. The following correspondence between self-identified race and
race defined by GLI classification will be used:
      Self-identified Race                         GLI-defined Race
      American Indian or Alaska Native             Mixed/Other
      Asian                                        South East Asian
      Black or African American                    African American
      Native Hawaiian or Other Pacific Islander    Mixed/Other
      White                                        Caucasian
      Unknown                                      Caucasian
      Not reported                                 Caucasian
      More than one race indicated                 Mixed/Other

5.3    HHD and Grip Strength
Manual muscle testing by hand-held dynamometry (HHD) assesses isometric muscle strength by
recording force generated against a hand-held dynamometer placed against a given limb by a
trained evaluator. Each muscle is tested with a standard beginning position, usually midway
between maximum flexion or extension of the muscle being studied, and with the dynamometer
placed at a defined position. Trained evaluators coach participants through 3 to 5 maximal
exertions for each muscle and record the maximum force observed in pounds. Evaluators note if
participants are not able to overcome the force exerted for a given muscle. Conversely, a value of
0 is recorded for a given muscle if the participant cannot assume the testing position due to
weakness or can attain the position but cannot exert measurable force.
The following 11 muscles are tested bilaterally by HHD: shoulder flexion, elbow flexion, elbow
extension, wrist extension, abductor pollicis brevis contraction, abductor digiti minimi
contraction, first dorsal interosseous contraction, hip flexion, knee flexion, knee extension, and
ankle dorsiflexion. Grip strength is tested bilaterally by Jamar hand dynamometer. Between
HHD and hand grip, 24 muscles or muscle groups are tested. The force recorded for each muscle
```

```text
Cudkowicz (PI) HEALEY ALS Platform Trial: Master Statistical Analysis Plan   Version 3.0, 06 Feb 2023


is converted to a percentage change from baseline among muscles with non-zero strength at
baseline. Individual percentages are averaged together to yield upper extremity, lower extremity,
and global average percentages. Higher values indicate greater strength retention.
HHD data will also be summarized as time to loss of strength. HHD0 and HHD0[2] will be
defined as the times from the Baseline Visit to the first and second post-baseline occurrence of a
muscle with a strength recording of 0, respectively, among those muscles that were non-zero at
baseline. All 11 muscles assessed by HHD as well as grip strength will be considered. Both
HHD0 and HHD0[2] are composite endpoints with death or death-equivalent, censored at the
Week 24 Visit if completed or at the earlier of the end of the Week 24 Visit window or date last
known alive if a Week 24 visit is not completed. Larger HHD0 and HHD0[2] times indicate
longer preservation of strength.

5.4   Biofluid Biomarkers
Blood and urine biomarkers will be assayed. Biomarkers will include serum creatinine and serum
and cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) neurofilament light chain (NfL). Serum creatinine will be assayed
by the kinetic Jaffe method (test 001370, Labcorp, Burlington, NC). NfL will be assayed by
single-molecule array (Simoa; Quanterix, Billerica, MA). Levels of serum and CSF NfL that are
reported to be below the limit of quantitation will be imputed at the limit of quantitation. Levels
of serum and CSF NfL will be log-transformed in all analyses. Other biomarkers may be
analyzed.

5.5   Patient-reported Outcomes
Patient-reported outcomes may be included as regimen-specific assessments. The description and
scoring of each instrument will be specified in the R-SAP.

5.6   Survival
The primary survival endpoint will be the composite of death or death equivalent. A secondary
survival endpoint of death alone independent of any death equivalent (including a death
equivalent that occurs prior to baseline) is also defined. Death equivalent will be defined as use
of permanent assisted ventilation (PAV), where PAV is defined as more than 22 hours per day of
noninvasive or invasive mechanical ventilation for more than seven consecutive days. The date
of PAV initiation is the first day of the consecutive days. The date of PAV initiation, where
applicable, will be imputed as the fifteenth day of a month if not specified more precisely. Any
participant on PAV at baseline will be censored at baseline for the composite survival endpoint.
Time at risk for both the composite survival endpoint of death or death equivalent and the
survival endpoint of death alone will be measured from each participant's Baseline Visit and will
be censored at the Week 24 Visit if completed or at the earlier of the end of the Week 24 Visit
window or date last known alive if a Week 24 visit is not completed.
Additional secondary analyses of survival may consider different durations of follow-up.

5.7   King's ALS Clinical Staging System
The King's ALS Clinical Staging System (Roche et al. 2012) is a 4-level ordinal scale with the
first three levels indicating the number (1, 2, or 3) of distinct central nervous system regions
```

```text
Cudkowicz (PI) HEALEY ALS Platform Trial: Master Statistical Analysis Plan       Version 3.0, 06 Feb 2023


(bulbar, upper limb, and lower limb) with neuromuscular dysfunction and levels 4a and 4b
indicating nutritional or respiratory failure secondary to ALS, respectively.
Participants will be classified to King's stage 1, 2, 3, 4a, or 4b based on scores from ALSFRS-R
assessments according to a published derivation (Balendra et al. 2014). Bulbar involvement is
defined as a score less than 4 on any of the ALSFRS-R questions in the bulbar domain (questions
1, 2, and 3). Upper limb involvement is defined as a score less than 4 on either of the ALSFRS-R
questions related to hand function (questions 4 and 5A). Lower limb involvement is defined as a
score less than 4 on the ALSFRS-R question about walking (question 8). Nutritional failure is
defined as responding that the participant uses gastrostomy for greater than 50% of their
nutrition. Respiratory failure is defined as a score of 0 on the ALSFRS-R question addressing
dyspnea (question 10 or R-1) or a score less than 4 on the ALSFRS-R question about use of
mechanical ventilation (question 12 or R-3). Participants without evidence by ALSFRS-R scores
of involvement of any of the three central nervous system regions will be scored as King's stage
1 due to their confirmed diagnosis with ALS. Participant may meet criteria for both King's stage
4a and 4b.

5.8     Clinical Safety Laboratory Tests
Clinical safety labs include hematology, blood chemistry panel, liver function tests, thyroid
function, urinalysis, and pregnancy testing in women of childbearing potential as specified in
Section 9.1.2 Clinical Safety Laboratory Tests of the Master Protocol:
x     Hematology with differential panel: complete blood count with differential (hematocrit,
      hemoglobin, platelet count, RBC indices, Total RBC, Total WBC, and WBC & differential)
x     Blood chemistry panel/Liver function tests (LFTs): alanine aminotransferase (ALT (SGPT)),
      aspartate aminotransferase (AST (SGOT)), albumin, alkaline phosphatase, bicarbonate, blood
      urea nitrogen, calcium, chloride, creatinine (estimated Glomerular Filtration Rate [eGFR]
      will be calculated using the MDRD equation and creatinine clearance will be calculated
      using the Cockcroft-Gault equation), glucose, magnesium, phosphate, potassium, sodium,
      thyroid-stimulating hormone (TSH), total bilirubin and total protein
x     Urinalysis: albumin, bilirubin, blood, clarity, color, glucose, ketones, nitrate, pH, protein,
      specific gravity, urobilinogen and WBC screen
x     Serum human chorionic gonadotrophin (hCG) for WOCBP (collected only at Master
      Protocol Screening Visit, and as necessary throughout course of study)
x     Coagulation parameters: Prothrombin time, International normalized ratio, Activated partial
      thromboplastin time


Clinical safety labs will also include derived measures of potential drug-induced liver injury
(DILI), including those that potentially meet the Hy’s law criteria, as distinct safety lab
outcomes.
Three potential DILI criteria will be defined:
x     ALT or AST >3x ULN with TBL >1.5x ULN
```

```text
6. Statistical Methodology

6.1     Analysis Sets
The following analysis sets will be used for testing efficacy, safety, and tolerability endpoints of
a specific focal regimen:
x     Efficacy Full Analysis Set (FAS): Participants who were randomized within the focal
      regimen plus placebo participants from specified regimens who had an opportunity to
      complete placebo-controlled follow-up, classified according to their randomized treatment
      assignment. Observations made after premature permanent discontinuation of study drug are
      included in this sample, should such participants remain on study. Observations completed
      after regimen data lock will be excluded. Participants determined to not meet ALS diagnostic
      criteria are excluded.
x     Efficacy Concurrent-controls (ECC) Set: The subset of participants in the FAS analysis set
      who were randomized within 180 days prior to the first randomization for the applicable
      regimen and within 180 days after the last randomization for the applicable regimen,
      exclusive.
x     Efficacy Regimen-only (ERO) Set: The subset of participants in the FAS analysis set who
      were randomized within a given regimen.
x     Efficacy Common Mode of Administration (ECM) Set: The subset of participants in the FAS
      analysis set who are in regimens in which study drug is administered by the same route as the
      focal regimen (including oral, subcutaneous injection, intravenous infusion).
x     Efficacy Per-protocol (EPP) Set: The subset of participants in the FAS analysis set who
      initiated study treatment and who meet other requirements for protocol compliance as
      specified in the R-SAP, classified according to the treatment they actually received. This
      sample may include placebo participants from no other, some other, or all other regimens and
      may be restricted to those who would have been eligible for the applicable regimen as
      specified in the R-SAP. If a participant's data are truncated for inclusion in the EPP analysis
      set due to a time-dependent event (e.g., non-adherence to protocol-specified dosing, initiation
      of a prohibited medication, initiation of a medication approved for treatment of ALS),
      clinical events observed after the censoring event may be included in the EPP analysis set as
      specified in the R-SAP. Data from placebo participants shared from other regimens will not
      be truncated due to non-adherence to protocol-specified dosing.
x     Safety Full (STF) Set: Participants who initiated treatment within the focal regimen plus
      placebo participants from specified regimens who are not known to be ineligible for the focal
      regimen and who initiated treatment in their respective regimen, classified according to the
      treatment they actually received. Observations made after premature permanent
      discontinuation of study drug are included in this sample, should such participants remain on
      study. Observations completed after regimen data lock are excluded.
```

```text
Cudkowicz (PI) HEALEY ALS Platform Trial: Master Statistical Analysis Plan    Version 3.0, 06 Feb 2023


x     Safety Narrow (STN) Set: The subset of participants in the STF analysis set who were
      exposed to interventions with the same mode of administration.
x     Safety Regimen-only (SRO) Set: The subset of participants in the STF analysis set who were
      randomized within a given regimen.
The meaning of regimen data lock and the criteria for inclusion of data for placebo participants
assigned to other regimens at the time of regimen data lock will be defined in the HEALEY ALS
Platform Trial Data Management Plan and the HEALEY ALS Platform Trial Data Sharing Plan.

6.2     Baseline Characterization
Each analysis sample will be summarized overall and by treatment group for the following
characteristics: randomization site; age, sex, race, ethnicity, El Escorial diagnosis, months since
ALS symptom onset, delay between symptom onset and ALS diagnosis, site of ALS symptom
onset, use at baseline of riluzole, edaravone, or sodium phenylbutyrate/taurursodiol and
combinations of use and non-use of these medications, ALSFRS-R total score, delta-FRS, SVC,
King's stage, weight, body mass index (BMI), serum creatinine concentration, and serum NfL
concentration.

6.3     Primary Efficacy Analysis and Supportive Analyses
Unless otherwise specified in the RSA, the primary analysis for each regimen is a Bayesian
shared-parameter model of function and survival. Details of the model, including documentation
of operating characteristics under a range of scenarios, are provided in the MPRDR (Appendix 1
to this M-SAP). Any regimen-specific deviations are described in the applicable RSA and R-
SAP.
The estimand of the primary analysis is the relative rate of disease progression (the "disease rate
ratio" or DRR) of active treatment relative to placebo in the FAS population under an assumption
that active treatment slows mean time to death or death equivalent by the same proportion as
treatment slows the mean rate of functional progression as measured by change in ALSFRS-R
total score over time. The estimand is defined by the following attributes:
x     Treatment: active treatment vs. placebo.
x     Population: FAS population as defined in Section 6.1.
x     Variables: time to death or death equivalent and rate of change in ALSFRS-R total score
      from baseline to the Week 24 Visit.
x     Intercurrent event 1: treatment discontinuation due to death or death equivalent: no ALSFRS-
      R data from participants who reach the death or death equivalent endpoint are included in the
      analysis, handled via mortality component in model, composite variable strategy approach.
x     Intercurrent event 2: treatment discontinuation not due to death or death equivalent: handled
      via treatment policy approach, all data will be used including data collected during the
      placebo-controlled period after treatment discontinuation regardless of concomitant
      medication, for those participants who have not been censored due to mortality. Missing data
      post-treatment will not be imputed, handled via missing at random assumption.
x     Population-level summary: mean ratio of hazard or progression rate of active treatment
      relative to placebo.
```

```text
Cudkowicz (PI) HEALEY ALS Platform Trial: Master Statistical Analysis Plan    Version 3.0, 06 Feb 2023


6.4     Interim Analysis
Unless otherwise specified in the RSA, regimens will be considered for early stopping for
success or futility at regular intervals. The default design for a regimen will include stopping
early only for futility. Details of the interim analysis schedule, the early stopping rules, and
documentation of operating characteristics under a range of scenarios, are provided in the
MPRDR (Appendix 1 to this M-SAP). Any regimen-specific deviations are described in the
applicable RSA and R-SAP.

6.5     Secondary Efficacy Analyses
6.5.1    Hierarchical Testing
Primary inference for secondary efficacy endpoints will be based on analysis of the FAS analysis
set using a repeated-measures linear mixed model for functional endpoints (see Section 6.5.2
below) and by Kaplan-Meier product-limit estimates and log-rank test for survival (see Section
6.5.5 below). The default sequence for testing is the following:
      1. Change from baseline to Week 24 in upper limb muscle strength as assessed
         isometrically using HHD and grip strength and measured as average percent change from
         baseline among maneuvers with non-zero strength at baseline in the FAS analysis set,
      2. Change from baseline to Week 24 in respiratory function as assessed by slow vital
         capacity (SVC) and measured as percent predicted using GLI normal values in the FAS
         analysis set,
      3. Change from baseline to Week 24 in lower limb muscle strength as assessed
         isometrically using HHD and measured as average percent change from baseline among
         maneuvers with non-zero strength at baseline in the FAS analysis set, and
      4. Survival defined as freedom from death and permanent assisted ventilation (PAV) from
         baseline to Week 24 in the FAS analysis set.
RSAs may specify a different sequence of secondary endpoints to evaluate using a closed testing
procedure or another method for controlling family-wise type 1 error rate.
If the primary analysis indicates a significant slowing in disease progression from the Bayesian
shared-parameter model of function and survival, then each secondary efficacy endpoint would
be declared significant in the specified sequence using a criterion of two-tailed p < 0.05. After
the first failure to declare significance, no endpoints lower in the hierarchy can be significant.
Nominal comparison-wise p-values for secondary efficacy endpoints will also be reported.
6.5.2    Repeated-measures Model
Treatment groups of a given regimen will also be compared for progression of continuous
efficacy and selected safety endpoints (ALSFRS-R total score, ALSFRS-R domain scores, HHD
upper extremity, lower extremity, and global average percentages, SVC percent-predicted, serum
creatinine, serum NfL, and weight) using a repeated-measures linear mixed model in the FAS,
ECC, ERO, ECM, and EPP samples. The model will include fixed terms for discrete visit,
treatment group, treatment group × visit interaction, time since symptom onset and time since
symptom onset × visit interaction, delta-FRS and delta-FRS × visit interaction, baseline log-
transformed serum NfL level and baseline log-transformed serum NfL level × visit interaction,
baseline riluzole use and baseline riluzole × visit interaction, baseline edaravone use and baseline
```

```text
and visit will be centered by subtracting the sample mean of a given covariate for a given
analysis set. The model will assume unstructured covariance among participant-specific repeated
measures. In analysis sets in which placebo participants are drawn from more than three
regimens, the model will include random regimen-specific intercepts. In analysis samples with
three or fewer regimens, the model will include fixed effects for regimen.

where Yij is a given efficacy endpoint measured for participant i at visit j, ak(i) is a random

transformed serum NfL level, centered baseline riluzole use, centered baseline edaravone use,
and centered baseline sodium phenylbutyrate/taurursodiol use) for participant i, ti is an indicator
variable for treatment t to which participant i was assigned, Ȗ1, Ȗ2,j, Ȗ3, Ȗ4,j, and Ȗ5,j are estimated

The following SAS code specifies the model:
proc mixed data=xxx method=reml;
  class regimen id trtrnd visit;
  model Value = trtrnd|visit
                sx2bl|visit dFRS|visit nfl|visit rlz|visit edv|visit
                amx|visit / solution cl;
  random intercept / subject=regimen type=vc;
  repeated visit / subject=id type=un;

where id is a participant study identifier, trtrnd is the randomly assigned treatment group,
visit is the visit identifier, Value is value of the efficacy endpoint being tested for a given
participant at a given visit, sx2bl is months since ALS symptom onset, dFRS is pre-baseline
slope, nfl is baseline log-transformed serum NfL level, rlz is an indicator of riluzole use at
baseline, edv is an indicator of edaravone use at baseline, and amx is an indicator of sodium
phenylbutyrate/taurursodiol use at baseline.
The primary estimate will be the treatment-dependent difference in change from baseline to the
Week 24 Visit. The estimate and its 95% Wald confidence bounds will be obtained by a linear
contrast of adjusted means. The following SAS code specifies the linear contrast for a regimen
with one active treatment assuming an endpoint measured every 8 weeks and that the sort order
for treatment group has the active group last and visits are sorted chronologically:
  estimate "3|Act vs Plb|dWk 24" trtrnd*visit 1 0 0 -1                        -1 0 0 1 / cl;
```

```text
Cudkowicz (PI) HEALEY ALS Platform Trial: Master Statistical Analysis Plan   Version 3.0, 06 Feb 2023


The estimand estimated by the primary linear contrast of the repeated-measures linear mixed
model is the mean difference in 24-week change from baseline of a given continuous efficacy
endpoint in the active treatment group relative to the placebo group in the FAS population. The
estimand is defined by the following attributes:
x   Treatment: active treatment vs. placebo.
x   Population: FAS population as defined in Section 6.1.
x   Variables: absolute change in endpoint from baseline to the Week 24 Visit.
x   Intercurrent event: treatment discontinuation: handled via treatment policy approach, all data
    will be used including data collected during the placebo-controlled period after treatment
    discontinuation. Missing data post-treatment, including data missing due to death, will not be
    imputed, handled via missing at random assumption.
x   Population-level summary: difference in conditional means of active treatment relative to
    placebo.
A significant positive difference in 24-week change from baseline in the direction of greater
improvement or less worsening among participants randomized to active treatment would
support inference of benefit from active treatment for the efficacy endpoint being tested.
Inference from this analysis is supportive of inference from the Bayesian shared-parameter,
repeated-measures model for the primary endpoint.
6.5.3   Random-slopes Model
Treatment groups of a given regimen will be compared for mean rate of progression of
continuous efficacy and selected safety endpoints (ALSFRS-R total score, ALSFRS-R domain
scores, HHD upper extremity, lower extremity, and global average percentages, SVC percent-
predicted, serum creatinine, serum NfL, and weight) using a random-slopes linear mixed model
in the FAS, ECC, ERO, ECM, and EPP samples. The model will include fixed terms for month
since the Baseline Visit, treatment group, treatment group × month interaction, time since ALS
symptom onset and time since ALS symptom onset × month interaction, delta-FRS and delta-
FRS × month interaction, baseline riluzole use and baseline riluzole use × month interaction,
baseline edaravone use and baseline edaravone use × month interaction, baseline sodium
phenylbutyrate/taurursodiol use and baseline sodium phenylbutyrate/taurursodiol × month
interaction, and baseline log-transformed serum NfL level and baseline log-transformed serum
NfL level × month interaction. Study months will be calculated as the difference in days from the
Baseline Visit to the date of assessment of a given endpoint multiplied by 12 / 365.25. Fixed
terms other than treatment group and month will be centered by subtracting the sample mean of a
given covariate for a given analysis set. The model will include random participant-specific
intercepts and slopes with unstructured covariance. In analysis samples in which placebo
participants are drawn from more than three regimens, the model will include random regimen-
specific intercepts and slopes with unstructured covariance. In analysis samples with three or
fewer regimens, the model will include fixed effects for regimen and regimen x month
interaction.
```

```text
Cudkowicz (PI) HEALEY ALS Platform Trial: Master Statistical Analysis Plan   Version 3.0, 06 Feb 2023
```

```text
A significant positive difference in slopes in the direction of greater improvement or less

from active treatment for the efficacy endpoint being tested. Inference from this analysis is
supportive of inference from the Bayesian shared-parameter, repeated-measures model for the
primary endpoint.
6.5.4   Survival
The survival endpoints will be analyzed in the FAS, ECC, ERO, ECM, EPP, STF, STN, and
SRO analysis sets. The proportion of participants who reach each survival endpoint and the time
from baseline to that endpoint will be summarized by treatment group in all analysis samples. If
more than 10% of a given sample reaches a survival endpoint, then the estimated time to a 10%
event rate will be estimated by Kaplan-Meier product-limit estimates with complimentary log-
log confidence bounds by treatment group. Time to each survival endpoint will be compared
between treatment groups by log-rank test. If at least one occurrence of a given survival endpoint
is observed in each treatment group, then the treatment-dependent hazard ratio with profile
likelihood confidence bounds will be estimated by unadjusted Cox proportional hazards
regression and by Cox proportional hazards regression adjusting for age at baseline, time since
ALS symptom onset, delta-FRS, baseline log-transformed serum NfL level, use of riluzole at
baseline, use of edaravone at baseline, and use of sodium phenylbutyrate/taurursodiol at baseline
with a random gamma-distributed regimen-specific frailty term. If convergence is not obtained
or the analysis sample includes 3 or fewer regimens, then the frailty term will be dropped.
6.5.5   CAFS
To account for potentially informative loss to follow-up due to death, treatment groups of a given
regimen will be compared by the Combined Assessment of Function and Survival (CAFS, Berry
et al. 2013) in the FAS, ECC, ERO, ECM, and EPP samples. CAFS is a joint-rank test
(Finkelstein and Schoenfeld 1999) of a composite endpoint combining function and survival.
The test consists of calculating a rank-sum score for each individual relative to pair-wise
comparisons with all other participants. Participants are ranked according to time to the survival
endpoint when that is observed for both members of a pair or when one is censored after the
observed event time for the other. Pairs that cannot be ranked by time to the survival endpoint
are ranked by absolute change from baseline in the functional endpoint at the maximum follow-
up time at which both participants have an observation. Pair-wise rankings are scored -1 for the
member of the pair with shorter time to the survival endpoint or greater decline in function and 1
for the other member or 0 for both members when time to the survival endpoint is equal or not
evaluable and change in function is equal, e.g., when one participant is lost to follow-up after
baseline. Pair-wise ranking scores are summed by participant to calculate a rank-sum. Inference
of treatment-dependent difference in the hierarchical composite of death and disease progression
is based on a Wilcoxon rank-sum test of the participant-specific rank-sum scores or on a
regression of the rank-sum scores adjusting for covariates.
```

```text
Cudkowicz (PI) HEALEY ALS Platform Trial: Master Statistical Analysis Plan   Version 3.0, 06 Feb 2023


All combinations of the following elements will be tested:
1. Survival endpoint: (a) death or death-equivalent, and (b) death alone.
2. Function endpoint: (a) ALSFRS-R total score, (b) HHD upper extremity percentage, (c) SVC
percent-predicted, and (d) HHD lower extremity percentage.
3. Analysis: (a) Wilcoxon rank-sum test by t-distribution approximation, and (b) adjusted mixed
model analysis controlling for time since ALS symptom onset, delta-FRS, baseline log-
transformed serum NfL level, use of riluzole at baseline, use of edaravone at baseline, and use of
sodium phenylbutyrate/taurursodiol at baseline and including a random effect of regimen in all
analyses that include at least three regimens or a fixed effect of regimen otherwise.
6.5.6   HHD0 and HHD0[2]
The time to first and second loss of measurable strength among muscles assessed by HHD and
grip strength will be compared between treatments by interval-censored nonparametric
maximum likelihood methods and interval-censored Cox proportional hazards regression in the
FAS, ECC, ERO, ECM, and EPP samples. The model will adjust for time since ALS symptom
onset, delta-FRS, baseline log-transformed serum NfL level, use of riluzole at baseline, use of
edaravone at baseline, and use of sodium phenylbutyrate/taurursodiol at baseline.
The first HHD0 and HHD0[2] analyses will consider all 11 muscles and grip strength with non-
zero strength at baseline (up to 24 bilateral strength assessments in total). A second pair of
HHD0 and HHD0[2] analyses will restrict to the four bilateral muscle groups or grip strength
that reach zero most frequently in a specific analysis sample (up to 8 bilateral strength
assessments in total).
6.5.7   Placebo Multiple Imputation
To account for possible treatment-dependent loss to follow-up whether due to death, intolerance,
or other causes, the effect of treatment on ALSFSR-R total score, HHD upper extremity
percentage, SVC percent-predicted, and HHD lower extremity percentage will be estimated in
the repeated-measures and random-slopes models using placebo multiple imputation for missing
data (Ayele et al. 2014) in the FAS and ERO samples. Fifty imputed datasets will be generated
for a given endpoint. Missing values for each visit will be imputed from those predicted by linear
regression from earlier visits using data only from placebo participants, adjusting for time since
symptom onset, delta-FRS, baseline log-transformed serum NfL level, baseline riluzole use,
baseline edaravone use, and baseline sodium phenylbutyrate/taurursodiol use.
The following SAS code specifies the imputation for an endpoint measured every 8 weeks:
proc mi data=work.outcomes seed=xxx nimpute=50 minmaxiter=1000
        minimum=. . . . . . . x x x x maximum=. . . . . . . y y y y ;
  class trtrnd rlz edv amx;
  fcs reg(Wk08 = sx2bl dFRS nfl rlz edv amx Wk00);
  fcs reg(Wk16 = sx2bl dFRS nfl rlz edv amx Wk00 Wk08);
  fcs reg(Wk24 = sx2bl dFRS nfl rlz edv amx Wk00 Wk08 Wk16);
  mnar model( Wk08 Wk16 Wk24 / modelobs=(trtrnd="0"));
  var sx2bl dFRS nfl rlz edv amx Wk00 Wk08 Wk16 Wk24;
run;
```

```text
Cudkowicz (PI) HEALEY ALS Platform Trial: Master Statistical Analysis Plan   Version 3.0, 06 Feb 2023


where Wk00, Wk08, Wk16, and Wk24 are the values of a given efficacy endpoint at the Baseline,
Week 8, Week 16, and Week 24 Visits, respectively, trtrnd has a value of zero (0) for
participants randomized to placebo, and x and y take appropriate values to specify the range of a
given outcome measure (i.e., 0 and 48 for ALSFRS-R total score; 0 and . [missing value
indicator] for HHD upper and lower extremity percentages and SVC).
6.5.8   Additional Sensitivity Analyses of Primary and Key Secondary Outcomes
To evaluate the influence of extreme values on treatment estimates from the random-slopes
model, a robust linear mixed model that down-weights extreme values (Koller 2013) will be used
as a sensitivity analysis of primary and key secondary outcomes using the FAS analysis set. The
same fixed and random effects as specified for the primary random-slopes model in Section 6.5.3
will be used. A smoothed Huber ȡ-function will be used with a tuning parameter that yields 95%
asymptotic efficiency for normally distributed data.
As an alternative to CAFS for controlling the influence of potentially information loss to follow-
up due to death or other causes, sensitivity of estimated treatment effects to potentially
informative missingness will be analyzed using a composite endpoint of time to death and 24-
week change in ALSFRS-R total score using the FAS analysis set with imputation across a range
of weights spanning relative benefit or harm from the active treatment by exponential tilting
(Wang et al. 2017).
6.5.9   Subgroup Analyses
Differences in treatment efficacy will be estimated in the following subgroups by including
subgroup, subgroup × time, and subgroup × treatment × time terms to random-slope models for
primary and secondary efficacy endpoints using the FAS analysis set .
x   Age (less than 65 years vs. 65 years or older),
x   Sex (female vs. male),
x   Race (white vs. any minority race with greater than 5% prevalence in the sample),
x   Ethnicity (Hispanic or Latino vs. non-Hispanic or Latino),
x   Baseline use of riluzole (yes vs. no),
x   Baseline use of edaravone (yes vs. no),
x   Baseline use of sodium phenylbutyrate/taurursodiol (yes vs. no),
x   BMI (less than 25 kg/m2, 25 to less than 30 kg/m2, 30 kg/m2 or more),
x   Chronic kidney disease (CKD) stage (stage 1 or better [eGFR 90 mL/min/1.73m2 or more],
    stage 2 [eGFR 60 to 89 mL/min/1.73m2], stage 3 or worse [eGFR less than 60
    mL/min/1.73m2]),
x   delta-FRS (fast vs. slow progression with median split),
x   Time since onset of weakness (less than 18 months vs. 18 months or longer),
x   El Escorial and time since onset of weakness (El Escorial definite and less than 18 months
    vs. not both),
```
